# M6 plan: the level ladder

The plan of record for [m6-the-ladder.md](m6-the-ladder.md) (the spec: goal, DoD, research). Section numbers are
stable: append, never renumber, so briefs can cite "§4.1". The process (§6) is M5's (m5-plan.md §6: M3's P1-P8 with
M4's amendments and M5 D18's model mix): one fresh lead session per item at high effort, slices under 300K of context
in their own worktrees, merges and doc passes on the cheapest model, one review per item, the live gate by the lead
alone, frames judged solo, a commit per slice through `tools/commit.ps1`. The rules are CLAUDE.md, "Hard rules" and
"Rules for agent sessions"; a brief cites them and restates nothing. M5's decisions are cited as "M5 D<n>"; this plan's
own are D1-D31 (§7), and the owner's later decisions from D32.

## 0. Status

Planned 2026-11-01 on master c6b4221a (M5 complete; the hero video merged), on the branch `m6/plan`. The owner's
decisions before planning are D1-D13 (from draft v2 and the strategist's memo, `cache/m6/m6-strategy.md` §6); the
questions asked at planning are D14-D16, answered the same day (the local model stays outside the agents' reach for
now, the two-week standing climb is on, item 0 starts at once); D32 (the same day, 14:00 UTC) runs only LM2 and LM1 on the local model and gives LM4's §9 sweep
and LM3's long-run measures to Claude (§9.1, §8); D46 (2026-11-03) makes the owner's later local-model jobs (LM5 and its
rerun, LM1 v2, LM2 v3's fact table, LM9 v2) drafts for items 3, 4, 5, 7, 8 and 9 (§2.2); the planner's defaults D17-D31 may be overruled at any
checkpoint. Budget (D22): 52.5% build plus 1.5% contingency over two meter weeks, about 2% of planning in the
week before; the meter read 82% of the week at planning, resetting 2026-11-02 09:59 UTC. **Item 0 is done** (2026-11-01:
the ladder as data, the quest loader, Winnie created and through the Academy and the Pathwarden, the outdoor fight, a fresh
climb from 1 to 10 in 92 minutes, cheat-free; §8). **Item 2 is done** (2026-11-02: the gear score, the floor of two
casters and a robe, the equip pass, the sale, the returns and the notes, the caster's mana rule; the climber bought her
Wand by play and read `casters=2`; §8). **Item 1 is done** (2026-11-03: the spell plan by the 95% rule, the credits
from level 1 and the spend, the scrivener catalog and the learn trip, the buy-trip, the VII loot rule and the picker by
band; the climber bought and learned Flame Bolt II at Holtburg by play on a fresh climb, `granted=0`; §8). Checkpoint 1
("It learns and it dresses") is reported. **Item 3 is done** (2026-11-04: the profile's new fields, the spine's seven tusker profiles and the three alternates, the
Holding's 30-minute read on BSD Mage at 300 kills an hour on half its slots; section 8). **Item 4 is done** (2026-11-04: the travel file, the portal hop and the rung-change station, the walk fixes, the learn and buy-trips by the Town Network; the hops proven live, the island leg live owed to item 7's gate A; section 8). **Item 5 is done** (2026-11-04: the quest profile whole, the step vocabulary and the headless Yes/No, the timers per character, the quest station and the shared step runner, the kill-task and trophy shapes, the nine tusk files, the autopilot's tusk errand, the Cave of Alabree; the builds proven live, the quests' live proofs owed to item 7, `m6-quest-loopbacks-wield`; section 8). Checkpoint 2 ("It travels and it quests") is answered (D63). **Item 6 is done** (2026-11-04: death on the ladder (the vitae ledger, the drop rule and `none-needed`, the corpse run by the travel file and `route=leg`, the retreat rule, the climb resumed) and the automation menu (the "Jobs" button, the job list, `/auto <task>`, the hand-back), the bind's `tied=skipped`, the castable bolt; the gate passed live, the island corpse run proven live, the outskirts loopback owed to item 7; section 8). **Item 7 is done** (2026-11-07: gate A: a fresh `Winnie` reached level 20 with `granted=0`, by the ladder's rung changes, and turned in the Female Tusk live; "one night" and "unattended" were not met: 33 hours and 21 restarts, the item's fixes between them; section 8). **Item 7's finish** (2026-11-08: retail's coin on a corpse by the server mod, D111; the owner's cap on Max Health and each attribute, D112; item 7's open rows, section 9's last block; the fresh 1-20 sprint is its own record: level 19 of 20 in one unattended night, 0 restarts, 1 death, the Female Tusk turned in; gate A's bar is still not met, section 8). **Items 8 and 14 and the player rules are merged to master unfinished** (2026-11-13, D134: item 8's six quests and the survival layer, item 14's buff bot and its build, the Abode from 40, D128-D133; section 8). **Next: the climb built out as Qwinnie climbs (D131), the stamina line (D133) and Alchemy by play (D132); the wand when she can wield it; then items 9, 11, 12.**

(Each lead appends its item's line to §8: what changed from the table, the agent size table, the gate numbers, the live
rounds, the mod installs, the spend by the meter.)

### 2.1 The leveling optimizer seat (D11, D18)

**What it owns:** the one plan from level 1 to 126 for a war mage, as data and a time model: the ladder file's rungs
and their order, the move-on rule per rung, the spell plan by level (the level, the element per ground, the scroll
source, the scarab stock), the skill and credit plan (the template's credits, no Alchemy (D34), the experience spend), the gear floor and
the quest kit, the quests that pay and when (the tusks by rung, the Atlan chain at base War 290, the round from 100),
and the hours per band with the measured and the modelled side by side. **It owns no code and runs no Godot or
backend**: it writes a memo and proposes data changes (a profile's value, a rung, a spell level) that a lead lands.

**Its form, across M6 and M7:** one seat because it is one plan. The plan sessions are **Fable 5.1 at high effort**
(the strategist's memo of 2026-10-31 is issue 1): issue 2 at checkpoint 3 (after gate A: the low rungs measured),
issue 3 after gate D (the Holding measured), issue 4 at M7's planning; about 2% of the meter each, from the
contingency. The reading is clerical: after every gate, soak and standing-job day, **the clerk reads the log with a
read-only script** (D32: the form of `cache/m6/longrun-measures/measure.py`; LM7 is not run) and the **Sonnet clerk** fills the measured column of `cache/m6/rates.md` (one
row per rung: modelled and measured seconds a kill, kills an hour, XP an hour, deaths an hour, mana wait, loot an hour,
the run and its date). The measured column is filled only from a log line, never from the model. A rung whose measure
misses its model by more than 30% is a finding (a check-act-check gap, a profile fault or a wrong assumption) named in
the next memo before the plan changes. The seat's calculator (the strategist's road model) lives under
`cache/m6/optimizer/`; issue 2 re-creates it there if it was left in a scratchpad.

### 2.2 The local model lane (D14, D20)

A local model (the owner's, at no meter cost) carries the high-volume, low-judgement, checkable work. **Every output is
a draft; a Claude agent verifies it before anything lands in code, a profile, a doc or a decision.**

- **The rule first (D14):** the model runs on another computer and the owner has granted no exception, so CLAUDE.md
  allows no agent to connect to it. **The lane runs by hand:** an agent writes a job's prompt under
  `cache/local-model/prompts/` (from `_rules.txt`), the owner runs it on the model's computer, the output lands in
  `cache/local-model/<job>/`, and a Claude agent verifies it. The seam below is the design kept for the day the owner
  grants a narrow exception for that one endpoint (its address out of tracked files); until then it is not built.
- **The seam (deferred by D14; item 0's slice lm is not built):** `tools/local-model.ps1 -Job <folder> [-Endpoint <from a gitignored file>]`, plain
  HTTP to the model's OpenAI-style `/v1/chat/completions` with `Invoke-RestMethod`, no NuGet package, no new tool
  install. The endpoint's address is read from `cache/local-model/endpoint.txt` (gitignored), never from a tracked file
  or a command line that a log keeps. A job is a folder under `cache/local-model/` with `job.md` (the prompt, built on
  `prompts/_rules.txt`) and `inputs.txt` (the file list); outputs go to the same folder with `progress.md` and
  `DONE.md` as the LM1-LM4 prompts already ask. The script resumes from `progress.md` and logs each request's size and
  time, never the content.
- **What it may never receive:** anything under `server\runtime\`; `secrets.json` or any file whose name holds
  "secret" or "lifestone-secrets"; anything under `.git`; the user profile; any file not on the job's allowlist. The
  allowlist is tracked files (`git ls-files`), the named `cache/` folders of the job (`cache/m6`, `cache/m5`,
  `cache/local-model`, a named soak folder) and the corpus; the script refuses any other path before it reads it. The
  owner's private files are never on an allowlist.
- **How a draft is checked:** a Claude agent (Sonnet; Haiku for mechanical counts) reads the job's `DONE.md` and its
  five "check first" items, then (1) every "closed" proposal and every number that will land in a profile or a quest
  file is checked against its cited source; (2) a 10% random sample of the rest is checked the same way; (3) the
  mechanical checks run: a drafted profile or quest file must load and pass its dat tests, a log digest's totals must
  match the run's `report.txt`. More than 10% wrong in the sample rejects the batch (re-prompted or done by Claude). The
  verifier writes `VERIFIED.md` in the job's folder (what was checked, what was wrong, what lands); nothing lands
  without it. Nothing of the corpus is committed (M5 D11).
- **The jobs (amended by D32).** The model runs **only LM2, then LM1**; LM3 and LM4 will not run, and LM5-LM8 are not
  written unless the owner hands the model more. LM4's sweep was done by the planner (§9.1); LM3's measures were pulled
  by a Claude agent with a read-only script (`cache/m6/longrun-measures/`, §8). The planned jobs fall to Claude: LM5's
  ground facts to item 3's research (Haiku lookups, each fact cited), LM6's tusk quests to item 5's, LM7's digest to
  the clerk's script (§2.1), LM8's doc sweep to item 12's doc pass. The original list, for the record: Handed over 2026-11-01 (none had written an output folder when this plan was written): **LM1** quest
  fact sheets (items 5, 7, 8), **LM2** ground fact sheets for the seed list's twelve grounds (item 3), **LM3** the long
  run's measurements (item 0: closes `m5-batch-figures`, `m5-leave-refresh-bound`, `m5-run-payback-live` and gives the
  rates file its den row), **LM4** the first-pass triage of §9's rows (item 0's sweep). Use what is done, check it, and
  never start a job that duplicates one still running. Planned, written by item 0's lead from the same template and
  handed to the owner to run by hand (D14): **LM5** ground facts for the grounds the strategist added (the Holding first, the Temple 20-40, the
  Cavern, the Emporium, Plated Rifts, Rampage Bluff, Vengeance Caverns, the Chapel of Mowen); **LM6** the island's tusk
  quests and Oolutanga's NPCs; **LM7** the standing digest of a gate or a soak log (counts, timelines, `Z ERROR `
  lines, anomalies) for the optimizer's clerk; **LM8** the doc consistency sweep at item 12 (stale names across docs:
  the old rungs, VII from Eastwatch, Num Lock).
- **The later jobs (D46, amends D32).** The owner ran more jobs through the model's job queue
  (`cache/local-model/queue/`, started by the owner, never by an agent: D14 stands). Their outputs are drafts for the
  items below and are checked as above, `VERIFIED.md` in each folder an item uses, before anything lands. An item's
  research starts from them and does its own lookups only for what they miss or get wrong; it does not redo a ground or
  a quest a checked draft already covers.
  - **LM2 v3** (`lm2-ground-facts-v3/`, the twelve seed grounds; the best of four runs, about 5 facts wrong in the
    Claude check, `compare/check/RESULT.md`) and **its fact table** (`lm2-ground-facts-v3-facts/facts.csv`: every number
    of the sheets as a row a script compares with the data; the rows that disagree are where a sheet is wrong): item 3.
  - **LM5** (`lm5-more-grounds/`: the Holding, the Temple 20-40, the Cavern, the Emporium, Plated Rifts, Rampage Bluff,
    Vengeance Caverns, the Chapel of Mowen, Oolutanga's Refuge, the War Room, the Abhorrent Vault, the Crystalline
    portal, the Elemental Caverns; the island's tusks in `tusks.md` and `tusks.csv`; its `DONE.md` names the five things
    to check first) and **LM5 v2** (`lm5-more-grounds-v2/`, an independent rerun, running on 2026-11-03): where the two
    runs agree a fact is trusted for the sample, where they disagree it is checked against the source first. Item 3
    (the grounds LM2 skips, the Holding first), item 4 (the routes and the water), item 5 (the tusks: LM6's work).
  - **LM1 v2** (`lm1-quest-facts-v2/`: 67 quest sheets, `index.csv`, `facts.csv`, `group-f-exclusions.md`): items 5, 7
    and 8 (the kill tasks at the rungs, the Eater jaws, the Shadow Stone, the Shield of Yanshi, the Atlan chain, the
    solo 80-126 timer quests and the ones excluded, each with its reason).
  - **LM9 v2** (`lm9-quest-census/batch-01` to `batch-20`, queued on 2026-11-03; one row per quest page, assembled by
    `queue/assemble-lm9.py`): item 9's catalog and daily round.
  - In a fact table, "right" and "wrong" count only the rows a script can compare with the data; the rest are marked
    to check by hand, and a queue fix round once moved rows out of the compared lane, so a clean table is not a check.

## 3. The waves

A wave is one to three items with a theme and a checkpoint; an item is one fresh lead session; a slice is one agent.
Shares are of a week on the owner's meter, item and lead together, from M5's as-run shares (a loop item with live
rounds 5-6%, a data item 3%, a mechanical item 2-4%).

| 5 | 11 | **Gate D, the Holding, and the standing job** (DoD 18): the first trip and the tie, the 10-hour soak at 80, 6 hours at 100 with VII and the wand; the standing job started (D15) | 14 M an hour or a named reason; item 7's memory and GC lines; 0 reconnects | 4% |
| 5 | 12 | **The end** (DoD 19): the cluster reviews, the audit table, §9 closed or re-placed, the doc pass, the rates file and the optimizer's memo, M7's seed, the hero (D9), the private push on the owner's word | the audit table complete | 3% |
| 6 | 14 | **The buff bot** (added 2026-11-11, D124-D127; section 3.1): a level VII buffer standing in Holtburg, built into the server mod; a tell with a profile's word (`tusker`, `tuskers`; since 2026-11-12 `all`, the default: section 3.1) casts that profile on the teller; the autopilot's buff-bot stop and the build that leans on it (Item Enchantment first, Mana Conversion next, Creature Enchantment last); an option of the autopilot, on by default | `BuffBotTests` (the mod), `BuffBotStationTests`, `SkillCreditTests` for the new order; live: a fresh climber from 1 to 20 with the bot on, read beside the long round of 2026-11-11, and the same climb with the bot off | 5% |
| | | **Build** | | **52.5%** plus 1.5% contingency = **54%** |

**Two meter weeks (D2):** week 1 is items 0-7 (32%), ending at checkpoint 3; week 2 is items 8-12 (20.5%). Each week
stays under the 45% stop line; the contingency pays the optimizer's issues 2-3. A lead asks before exceeding its item's
share by 20% (M3 D5), and each checkpoint re-estimates the waves left.

**The cut line for 80-126 (D3, the owner's named first cut):** item 11 whole (4%) and the Holding's profile and read in
item 3 and the VII rule in item 1 (about 1%): **about 5% saved; M6 ends at "80 reached, the den as home", M7 opens with
the Holding.** The buy-trip stays (D12); the round stays (gate E runs on a built character).

Order: 0 → (1 and 2 in parallel) → 3 → (4 beside 3's profiles, then 5) → 6 → 7 → checkpoint 3 → (8 and 9 in
parallel) → 10 → 11 → 12. Item 1 and 2 need item 0's ladder and character; item 3's fields come before item 4's
stations; item 5 needs item 0's quest loader and item 3's grounds; item 6 needs 1-5; item 7 needs 6; item 8 needs 5
(the shapes) and 4 (travel); item 9 needs 5 and 8 (the round runs the fetches); items 10 and 11 need everything; item
12 is last. Each later wave is sliced by its first lead at the wave boundary (M3 P8), in the shape of §4, with at most
four owner questions in one call.

### 3.1 Item 14 The buff bot (added 2026-11-11; branch `m6/14`, cut from `m6/8` on 2026-11-11 at the owner's word to build it now: item 8's merge waits for the long round, and item 14 merges after it)

**What it is.** In retail a buff bot was another player's character run by a script: a tell with a word, and it cast a
set of buffs on the teller. Lifestone has one player, so the bot is built in (D124): a mage the server mod places in
Holtburg, who hears a tell and casts through ACE's own spell code, with the cast's motion, words and effect. It is an
option of the autopilot, on by default, and the player can turn it off (D125). It is a Sho in Koujia armour (D126).

**The rule it keeps (D127).** The bot casts only what one player can cast on another: the Other spells of Creature
Enchantment and Life Magic at level VII (each attribute, each mastery, Armor, the protections, the three regenerations),
each for the spell's own hour. An Impenetrability or a bane cast at another player lands on that player's shield and on
nothing else (ACE `WorldObjects/WorldObject_Magic.cs` 2096-2140 @95ef426d: the self branch takes every worn piece, the
other branch the first enchantable shield, else "fails to affect"), so the climber's robe stays her own to bane. That
is what makes the build of this item: Item Enchantment first (her banes and Lifestone Recall), Mana Conversion next,
Creature Enchantment last (the bot covers what it bought).

**What the numbers say (read 2026-11-11 from the spell tables; slice S0 checks each).** A level VII attribute or
mastery line is +40, so War Magic gains about 60 (the mastery, and a quarter of Focus and Self): one bolt level sooner
all the way (III at about level 6, IV at about 15). Armor VII is +225 and Bludgeoning Protection VII leaves 0.35 of a
blow. The climber's own buff pass, which passes her regeneration from the Burrow's gate to level 32 and drank fifty
Mana Potions at one start of the long round, shrinks to her item lines. A level VII line lasts one hour.

**Slices** (each ends with `slice-check`; the mod slices are the lead's to install, `-ModOnly`, at a natural stop of
any live game):

| Slice | Content | Check |
|---|---|---|
| S0 | **Facts, read-only.** The level VII Other spell of every profile line, by id, with its duration and what it stacks over (a higher line of a category replaces her lower one); ACE's path for a creature casting a helpful spell at a player (the resist step, the registry, the duration); the mod's way to place a world object and to hear a tell with no database write; the Koujia pieces and a Sho body by SELECT on the offline world copy; a standing spot by Holtburg's lifestone that no walk route of the travel file crosses (a dat check); `SkillCreditPlan.Schedule` for the order Item Enchantment, Mana Conversion, Creature Enchantment; the spell plan's bolt level by level with the bot's lines up | a fact table under `cache/m6/14/`, every row with its source |
| S1 | **The bot in the mod.** `BuffBot`: placed when the mod is ready and removed at the stop, no database write; the profiles as data (`tusker` and `tuskers` first: every attribute, every mastery she can use, Armor, Bludgeoning Protection, Regeneration, Rejuvenation, Mana Renewal; `help` lists the words); a tell from a player within reach starts the profile, one cast after another at a real caster's pace; a tell from too far, an unknown word, or a second teller is answered by a tell that says why; a word for banes answers that only a shield takes them | `BuffBotTests` in the server mod's tests: the profile parse, the reach rule, the order, each refusal's reply; loopback: one tell, every line of the profile on the teller |
| S2 | **The stop at the bot.** The option (`OptionCatalog`: on by default; the dial's job list shows it and switches it); a journey station: check before (the bot stands at home; what her lines have left), walk to within reach, the tell, each enchantment read as it lands, check after (every line of the profile up with most of its hour), one named recovery (the tell once more), then her own pass, loud; the refresh rule (a way home with a bot line under ten minutes stops at the bot; in the field a line about to lapse is a trip home like a supply line); her own pass skips a line the bot's stands over | `BuffBotStationTests`; the `[ladder]` line reads `buffbot=on` or `off` |
| S3 | **The build that leans on it.** The credit order by the option, as data (a fresh climber only: a character keeps what she trained); the experience shares (Mana Conversion's ranks); the rung gates' second form (the bot's lines up, her own Impenetrability and bane at the level she reaches, and a castable Lifestone Recall: `m6-gate-recall-castable`); the first scrolls (no Armor Self and no protection scroll is bought; the item lines come first) | `SkillCreditTests`, `RungGateTests`, `SpellPlanTests` for both forms; with the option off every test of today passes unchanged |
| S4 | **The look.** A Sho mage in Koujia armour with a name, an idle stance, and the cast's motions seen from the player's camera; frames at 800x600 for the owner; anything that stands in for final art is said to be a placeholder | frames judged by the owner |
| S5 | **The gate.** A fresh climber from 1 to 20 with the bot on, beside the long round of 2026-11-11 (level by the hour, deaths, kills an hour, the share of time at rest, potions drunk); the same build with the bot off | the `[ladder]` lines of both; the record in section 8 |

**Rules this item touches.** The bot is a creature the mod places, not an account and not a character, so CLAUDE.md's
accounts rule is unchanged; the mod's install is `setup-server.ps1 -ModOnly` (pre-authorised) and a backend restart,
which a live game does not survive, so it waits for a natural stop. The climber is still granted nothing (D10, D34):
a buff is cast on her, nothing is given to her.

**What slice S0 found (2026-11-11; the fact table is `cache/m6/14/s0-facts.md`) and S1 built on.**

- **The bot hears a direct talk, not a tell by name.** ACE sends a tell by name to online players only; a talk to an
  object's id runs that creature's own answer (ACE `Network/GameAction/Actions/GameActionTalkDirect.cs` 21-50 @95ef426d).
  So the client talks to the bot by its id (slice S2 adds the action), and the bot's id is fixed: a static id of
  Holtburg's landblock that the world database does not use. ACE saves no static object that did not come from the
  database, so the bot is in no save.
- **No patch, so its answers are data.** The mod patches nothing in ACE and ACE calls no mod code on a talk. The bot is
  one of ACE's own creatures made from a weenie held in memory, and each answer is an emote set on that weenie (a
  direct talk whose text is the word): turn, tell, the casts, tell. ACE's emote manager paces the casts by their
  wind-up and drops a second talk while an answer runs. D138 changes this: each answer is now a hook (a stamp on the
  bot's own quest registry, then ACE's `Activate` emote on the bot itself, the mod's own subclass of ACE's creature), so
  the mod's code answers, charges, casts by a player's rules and walks to the shop; still no patch.
- **Two differences from a player's cast, both named TODOs (both closed by D138):** ACE's emote cast says no spell words
  (`m6-buffbot-spell-words`) and checks no range, so the reach is ACE's own for a direct talk, the teller's landblock
  and the ones beside it (`m6-buffbot-reach`); the autopilot walks to five metres first. The refusals S1's row names for
  a tell from too far and for a second teller are therefore ACE's (no answer), not the bot's own lines.
- **The spells** are the Other spell of each line at level VII, one id under the Self spell of the same line and in its
  category (`BuffBotSpellsDatTests`, on the dats). ACE stacks by category and power, so the bot's line stands over hers
  and a cast of her own under it is spent for nothing: the client must read a line as up by category and power
  (slice S2), or her pass recasts under the bot's and the gates read the lines as down.
- **His name** in `BuffBotWire.Name` is a placeholder until the owner names him.

**The second profile, `all` (the owner, 2026-11-12).** The tuskers' profile holds one protection, Bludgeoning, and a
creature with piercing and lightning attacks killed her in 50 seconds under it. For a run through the Direlands at
level 40, where every damage type is met, the bot knows a second word, `all`, and she says it by default.

- **Its lines (28).** Every line of the tuskers' profile, first and in its order, so whatever reads a line of the
  tuskers' (the rung gates, the Strength floor, the start's read) finds it in both. Then the six other elemental
  protections (Blade 2150, Piercing 2160, Fire 2156, Cold 2154, Acid 2148, Lightning 2158), Magic Resistance 2280,
  Impregnability 2242 and Arcane Enlightenment 2194. The ids are the world copy's spell table's, read 2026-11-12,
  and `BuffBotSpellsDatTests` proves each from the dats.
- **The mechanism is the tuskers', unchanged.** The bot casts the Other spell of the line at level VII, one id under
  the line's Self spell and in its category, for the spell's own hour. The client reads a line of the bot's by that
  spell's id in her enchantments (`BuffBotRule.Read`), and her own pass reads a line as up by category and power, so
  it never recasts under the bot's. She holds no line of the six protections, so each goes by the dat's name of its
  Self line ("Piercing Protection Self"); the three skill lines are lines of her own profile.
- **Left out, by name.** Mana Conversion Mastery is not left out: it is the tuskers' Mana Mastery line. Deception
  Mastery: no rule of hers reads Deception. Healing, Leadership, Fealty, the assessment, weapon and trade skills: not
  a mage's. Impenetrability and the banes: D127, her armour lines stay her own. Arcane Enlightenment is in because
  one rule of hers reads Arcane Lore (an item's spells need it to light).
- **The word is an option.** `--autopilot-buff-bot-word`, default `all`; `tusker` is still accepted and asks for the
  profile of before. Every rule reads the profile the word asks for: the stop is due when a line of it is down or has
  under 45 minutes left and is done only when every line of it is up; the trip home (7 minutes) and its urgent form
  (4 minutes) key on the least seconds any of its lines has left; the bot's cover is its lines. The catalog sells none
  of the nine new lines and no gate names one, so what she learns and buys is the same under both words.
- **How long the stop takes.** ACE paces an emote cast by the wind-up and the follow-through. Live (2026-11-12 00:49
  UTC) the tuskers' 19 lines took 28 s from the word: 2 s to the first, 1.5 s a cast. At that pace the 28 lines of
  `all` are about 42 s, 13 s more; this is computed, not yet seen live. The station allows 8 s a line and 30 s more
  (254 s for 28 lines), which still leaves the first line fresh when the last lands.
- **A mod that does not know the word.** A server mod installed before this change answers `all` with its help line
  and casts nothing. The stop then fails at once by name (`word-unknown`), her own pass runs, and no second tell
  waits out the answer's time. The mod must be installed again for `all` to be cast.

**The den run (2026-11-12, `m6-den-run`; branch `m6/14-denrun`).** The owner's decision: at level 40 she makes one
run to a Black Spawn Den's entrance, light and fully buffed, never stopping to fight; goes in, waits a minute, comes
out by the den's own exit, ties her portal recall at the entrance and goes home. After it the den is reached by
Primary Portal Recall.

- **The order.** `--autopilot-den-run` (off by default; the task `den-run` of the level up job; `tools/climb.ps1
  -DenRun`). In order: one forced trip home with its sale (the learn plan reads Primary Portal Tie and Primary Portal
  Recall first while the order stands; the trip ends at home), the stop at the buff bot when a line is due, her own
  pass, the check, the run, the stop. It stops `den-run:tied`, or by the failure's name under `den-run:`. The level
  stop rule is not read while the order stands.
- **The refusals, each by name, before the run:** `den-run:refused:level:<n><40`, `bot-off`, `bot-word:<word>` (the
  word must be `all`), `bot-lines:<up>of<n>` (a line down or under 45 minutes), `no-spell:<spell>` (Primary Portal
  Tie, Primary Portal Recall, Lifestone Recall), `burden:<percent>` (100% of the capacity or over: ACE's
  `EncumbranceSystem.GetBurdenMod` slows a body from 1.0), `not-home`.
- **The den.** The only portal chain open to a level-40 character that ends within 2.5 km of an entrance leads to the
  entrance at 60.0S 57.6W (portal 7246, landblock 0x3734), whose den is landblock 0x0103. It is the Aluvian den's
  copy: the same 653 cells on the dats and the same world rows but for the heritage chest (3994 for 3985) and the
  Surface Portals (7247 for 7243) (`DenCopyDatTests`, `DenCopySqlTests`). Its profile is `black-spawn-den-sho.json`:
  the Aluvian profile with the landblock read as 0x0103, its own tie record and entry (7246) and its exit (7247,
  which drops 27 m from the entrance). The name Sho is the file's, from the chest's weenie. The den's first exit
  stands behind no-go cells, so a leg to the exit takes back the two between it and the arrival's hall
  (`GroundRooms.ExitWay`: 0x0343 and 0x035C; 19.6 m from the arrival, walked on the dats); the fight's walks do not.
- **The route** (`travel-routes.json`, route `den-run` of `black-spawn-den-sho`, taken only by name): the Town
  Network; Danby's Outpost (portal 43003); 253 m on foot to the Desert March Portal (9685), across about 96 m of
  shallow water; inside its dungeon (0x02AA) 828 m over 102 rooms, down five storeys, to the Black Hill portal (2328),
  with 21 of the dungeon's 61 creature rows standing in rooms of the way (levels 30 to 80); then the run.
- **The run is not straight.** The straight 1,395 m crosses ground too steep to stand on from 694 m and the body's
  physics refuses it 692 m along. By one bend, at (40.5, 120.5) in 0x3932, both halves are straight lines the physics
  confirms: 956.8 m, a turn of 30.7 degrees, 486.8 m; 1,443.6 m in all. Encounter rows of the Obsidian Outer Mix
  Generator within 25, 40 and 60 m: 6, 12 and 22 (the unwalkable straight line: 5, 10, 14).
- **Run only** (`JourneyFacts.RunOnly`, `TravelLegRunOnly.cs`). No walk stops for an attacker, none fights back and
  none retreats; the survival layer and the vitals guard hold nothing. The one thing done for her health is a health
  drink on the move (ACE refuses a drink only to a body that is busy, teleporting or in the air: Food.cs:47-57). She
  casts nothing on the road: a cast needs her standing, and a recall teleports 2 s after its cast lands
  (WorldObject_Magic.cs:1136-1141). The overland legs are walked straight, in pieces of 96 m that the physics checks
  before each step; a piece it refuses hands the rest of the leg to the planner, by name.
- **One attempt an order.** A death while the run's station runs ends the run (`den-run:died:<stage>`): one line
  says where and by what, no death station is entered, the corpse is not fetched. A run that was cut is not begun
  again (`den-run:cut`). A relaunch with the switch is a new order.
- **Not seen live.** Everything above is from the dats, the world copy and ACE's source. Open, by name:
  `m6-den-run-live` (the first live run), `m6-den-run-drink-feet` (whether the body keeps its feet under the server's
  Drink motion), `m6-den-run-ladder` (the ladder file names no den rung for `black-spawn-den-sho` yet).

**Left as TODOs (section 9):** `m6-buffbot-real-character`, `m6-buffbot-price`, `m6-buffbot-profiles` (still open:
a profile by ground and by element),
`m6-buffbot-towns`, `m6-buffbot-spell-words`, `m6-buffbot-reach`.

### 3.2 Item 16 The one engine (added 2026-11-17; slices cut from `m6/14`, D139)

**What it is.** The automation's decisions each get one owner: a bestiary, a threat board, the survival layer over the
fight too (D119's stages 2 to 4), one combat entry with a brief, a quartermaster, and a thin director. The design, the
method (a strangler; shadow first; ratchets), the stages E0 to E7 and their proofs are in
[docs/automation-engine.md](../automation-engine.md); this section holds only the order and what is done.

**Order.** E0 (the document, D139, the ratchets, the baseline), then E0b (the climb headless) and E1 (the bestiary)
side by side; lane A (E2, E3, E4) and lane B (E5) after; E6; E7, whose gate is the fresh climber's run to 80.

**Done.** E0: `EngineRatchetTests` (14 concerns, 16 tests) and `engine-ratchets.json`.

## 4. Wave 0's slice table

Every slice: a fresh agent (the model by D23), 60-120 calls, under 300K of context, in its own worktree cut from the
item branch `m6/<item>` (`.claude/worktrees/m6-<item>-<slice>`, `git config core.longpaths true`); at most four files
to create and eight to edit; one acceptance command (`slice-check.ps1 -MinTests <count> -Steps <steps> -Rerun`, with
`-Exhaustive` for a slice that touches `src/Lifestone.Data` or the Client folders M4 D30 names); one Godot step at most;
the handoff file `docs/handoff-m6-<item>.md` in the branch (removed before the merge). A new judge or verdict field is
report-only in the slice that adds it. The test baseline is the count `slice-check.ps1` reports on master at the item's
start (M5's end: 11,674 default and 775 Exhaustive; M4 D28: a floor only the lead lowers, for deletions it names).

### 4.2 Item 1 Spells by level, the buy-trip and the VII rule (branch `m6/1`, cut from master 00184d7c)

Sliced by item 1's lead from a read-only map (`cache/m6/1/map.md`, 2026-11-02; the world rows in `cache/m6/1/sel-*.tsv`,
read offline from the pinned world SQL that `WorldSqlDump.cs` names, D28). The map's facts the slices rest on: **the
scroll shops are not the spec's** (Holtburg's scriveners sell every school I-IV on foot; Fort Tethana sells only VI, at its
Grand Master Scriveners; Sanamar sells I-IV; level V is sold only at Plateau Village, Qalaba'r and Dryreach, all on the Town
Network, and Westwatch; the spec §5.5 and the strategy §1.4 are corrected by the doc pass); ACE's 95% line is 43 over the
spell's power, not 42 (`SkillCheck.GetMagicSkillChance`, `WorldObjects/SkillCheck.cs:19-22` @95ef426d: +42 is 0.9498);
the client already has the formula (`GearCasts.Chance`, `Scenarios/GearScenario.cs:1253`); the climber spends experience
only on a logistics trip and trains nothing from the levels' credits (61,527 XP and 13 credits unspent at level 10 in item
0's climb; `IActionApi.TrainSkill` exists, only the build scenarios call it); a scroll's spell id is on the wire without an
appraisal (`WeenieDesc.SpellId`) and ACE always lets a character read a VII scroll (`CanReadScroll`,
`WorldObjects/Player_Skills.cs:458-471`); no station walks a Town Network hop yet (item 4's), so the climber's V, VI and
buy-trip towns are loud stops until it lands (D40). `Winnie`'s next level by the rule is II (War Magic 106 buffed at level 10:
II at 0.980), sold in Holtburg on foot (20216, the Flame Bolt II scroll 5998), so the item's live proof needs no hop (D42).

### 4.3 Item 2 Dressed by rule (branch `m6/2`, cut from master 00184d7c; §4.2 is left to item 1)

Sliced by item 2's lead from a read-only map (`cache/m6/2/map.md`, 2026-11-02). The map's facts the slices rest on: Holtburg's
Archmage Cindrue (2304) sells the plain Wand (2472) at 270 (the pinned world SQL's create list; the ladder note §11 and the
corpus's Holtburg page are wrong), so the vendor floor needs no trip; the climber runs no loot today (`tools/lib/Climb.psm1`
passes no `--fight-loot`, so the resupply trip's gear phase never runs for her); `Winnie` holds one caster (the Training Wand
12748); a floor line must not enter `supplies.kit` (`GearKit.HasNoKit` would flip and the kit-less death's
`rearm=corpse/none` would become a kit trip); ACE refuses to drop an attuned piece (`Player_Inventory.cs:1389-1393`, :866-871
@95ef426d) and the Pathwarden pieces are Value 0, so "returned or dropped" is returned to Thorolf by his Give emotes, else kept.
Every number from the world data is read from the pinned world SQL (the zip `WorldSqlDump.cs` names; D28) and cited by row.

## 5. Gates and the live-gate rules

M5's §5 in full (the four-lane parallel gate, at most three Godot windows, the journeys judged by their done lines,
G-A as `slice-check -Gate`, fresh journey characters each gate, the autopilot lane after the mage's journey), with these
M6 changes:

1. **Branches and worktrees:** `m6/<item>`, `.claude/worktrees/m6-<item>-<slice>`; the merged item's worktrees and
   branches removed after its merge (D24).
2. **The checkpoint after every wave** (M5 D17's shape): the wave's last lead writes the spend per item against §3, the
   DoD items met, the rates measured, the frames, and pings the owner; nothing is cut or started until the owner answers.
3. **The climber's runs (D25):** `Winnie` runs alone on the `test` account after the gate's lanes, never beside BSD
   Mage (one character per account in the world); a climb is judged on its `[ladder]` line, report-only in its first
   gate (D27), judged from the next item's gate: `granted=0` as a fact, the level reached, the rung, deaths recovered.
4. **The ladder's gates (A-E, the spec DoD 12, 15-18):** each alone on the machine, the display held on (the gate holds
   it itself), the backend under the lead, the Godot play host at 800x600 unless the gate's row says otherwise, looting
   on (M5 D155), recording off, the `[soak]` line and `tools/soak-watch.ps1`; a frame every 30 minutes checked for held
   items and models before it is shown; at most one re-run after a fix, then the numbers into §8 and the owner asked.
   Every gate's log goes to the optimizer's clerk (§2.1).
5. **The proof of 80-126 (D19):** gate D (the Holding: the first trip, the 10-hour soak at 80, 6 hours at 100), gate E
   (the round at 100), the rates file's measured rows, and the standing job (D15): the whole climb on the owner's
   machine after gate D, read daily; its result is M7's, its first week is evidence for M6's audit.
6. **Rounds, not marathons:** at most three live rounds per item, then the lead stops, writes the numbers into §8 and
   asks the owner.
7. **The owner's eye on every frame:** the lead checks held items and models before sending and says what is a
   placeholder; the frames that could serve the hero are copied to `cache/m6/hero/` (D29).

## 6. The rules and the process

The rules are CLAUDE.md, "Hard rules" and "Rules for agent sessions"; a brief cites them and restates nothing. The
templates are M5's (`cache/m5-prompt-templates.md`, T1-T7), copied by item 0's lead to `cache/m6-prompt-templates.md`
with the names changed and the rules block compared with CLAUDE.md's. The process decisions are M3's P1-P8 with M4's
and M5's amendments (the checkpoint after every wave, a planned research phase per item, the roadmap line at every
transition, frames judged solo, the away protocol: one go-ahead per item). Two rules are M6's own, from the spec's §4:
a rung-, ground- or quest-specific fact is a profile value, and a lead refuses a slice that adds one as a constant;
every number a profile or plan carries from the corpus has its SELECT or its cited dat read (D28).

T1 (the item lead prompt, with its settings table) is written by each lead for the next item into `cache/m6/<n>/`
before it ends. The local model's jobs (§2.2) are written from `cache/local-model/prompts/_rules.txt`.

## 7. Decisions

D1-D13 are the owner's, given before planning (draft v2 of 2026-10-27 and its §9 notes of 2026-10-30 and 2026-10-31,
and the rulings of 2026-11-01 recorded in the strategist's memo §6; recorded, not quoted). D14-D16 are the questions
asked at planning. D17-D31 are the planner's defaults, which the owner may overrule at any checkpoint.

She stops to fight back only an attacker that keeps pace and hits her again, one in her way when she has not moved for three seconds, a thrower or caster whose measured damage over the seconds left in its range would bring her to the retreat line, and, at the door before a portal, whatever is still on her. A creature's speed is its motion's run from the dats times the run rate of its Run skill times its scale (ACE's monster speed); hers is her live Run skill's. The retreat line, the mana line and the recall reserve recall her on the run as in a fight. The crossing's cost now prices a run past: a fight only with what keeps pace, the hits taken and the heals they cost, and a creature she can neither outrun nor kill counted as a recall. By these facts the island's tuskers run 14 to 22 m/s against her 7.7, so on the Holding's 1,507 m only the Stinging Chitticks are passed and the approach stays held (about 29 fights, 1,434 mana of bolts and 408 of heals: she would reach the portal about 1,100 mana short of the reserve); the Barracks' 20 m stays paid; the 449 m from Stonehold to the Phyntos Menace is paid (about one fight, 60 mana, 356 left at the portal), its golems passed. Unproven live: the speeds, the keep-pace call on real hits, the door fight, and the corpse leg. |

From level 60 a round costs one credit; a credit is one Platinum Scarab handed to the bot by a player's give (ACE takes one of a stack a give), and the bot tells the count; under 60 a round is free. The bot casts from its own mana, with a fizzle chance from its own skill against the spell's power, and burns the Platinum Scarab and the Prismatic Tapers of its foci formula from its own pack by ACE's player rule; a scarab given joins its pack. Under 100 tapers it finishes its round, waits ten seconds (the customer's time to finish), says in local chat that it is out of tapers, walks to Archmage Cindrue, sells the scarabs over its keep of 10 at her price, buys tapers up to 1,000 and walks back; a word told while it is away is answered on its return. Its pack, purse and credits live in a mod state file under `server/runtime`, starting from a monarchy's outfit (500 tapers, 10 scarabs, 50,000 pyreals). The climber carries Platinum Scarabs from 60 on every ground (a town stock line at Uziz, 12 kept, 4 the trip line: four stops an hour at most) and hands one over when she holds no credit; when she cannot pay, her own pass covers the lines. Closes `m6-buffbot-price`, `m6-buffbot-spell-words` and `m6-buffbot-reach`; opens `m6-paid-bot-live` and `m6-paid-bot-renew` (section 9). |
| D139 | One engine drives her | item 16 (new); every item's automation; completes D119 | **Each decision of the automation gets one owner** (the owner, 2026-11-17): the mana work, the fight, the loot, the sale and the wear were built again inside each process (the quest, the walk, the climb), and that stops. One combat process that knows why she fights (a hunt, a defence, a pass-through that runs past, D137); one master process for what she carries, buys, sells and wears; a database of every creature kind's capabilities for the fight; tasks as subroutines that state what they want. Built now, in stages beside the live climb, each new decider report-only for a gate before it takes over and each stage deleting the copy it replaces; repository tests hold every concern at its count (`EngineRatchetTests`). The fresh climber's run from creation to 80 is made on the engine and is its last gate; the hero video is recorded from it. The melee and archer paths stay as they are. The design, the stages and the counts: [docs/automation-engine.md](../automation-engine.md). |

## 8. As run

**Planning, D32 (2026-11-01).** The long run's measures (`cache/m6/longrun-measures/`, a Sonnet agent's read-only
script over the three logs; `SUMMARY.md`; the planner checked five figures at their lines): 10.13 h, 1,910 kills
(188.6 an hour), 5.59 M XP an hour at level 82-84, 0 deaths, 13 trips; a batch line 1.94 s median (2.40 max), 0 lapses;
the leave bound of 120 s never hit (worst 17.7 s); sell trips 77-130 s (median 83 s); walking 12.6% to 27.3% (402 of
573 legs were cancelled and log no seconds); the mana dance 4.9% of fight time. Its findings are new rows of §9
(`m6-buff-recovery-batch`, `m6-trip-mean-empty`, `m6-trip-mean-resume`, `m6-walk-cancel-seconds`); the den row seeds
`cache/m6/rates.md` in item 0. The §9 sweep is §9.1. (Each lead appends its item's record below.)

read from the paper doll and `gear=wielded`, not from the figure.

Stretches: 1 frozen tree to 15; 2 the Lodge twice, the owner closed the client on the unload race; 3 holding outside to
16; 4-9 the kit and the first full pass; 10-12 Recall and the home routes; 13 the Lodge with the kit; 14-16 the first
long fights, to level 20; 17-20 the tusk's pull, the trips; 21 the turn-in; 22 the way out.

Mod installs: one, the unload guard, `-ModOnly` from the item worktree, `LIFESTONE_ACE_UNLOAD_RACE=ace` restores retail's
race. Spend by the meter: 47% at the start (2026-11-05 01:30 UTC), 50% at 03:26, 73% at 2026-11-06 23:50, 78% at
2026-11-07 06:27 (the week resets 2026-11-09 10:00 UTC): item 7 about 31 points of its 3%, an overrun reported to the
coordinator along the way, kept going by the owner's direction and D63's line of 90%. Flakes seen: the frame window under
contention in the three-Godot lanes, reported only. Lessons: a stop must alarm by the process ending, not a log line; a
watcher's filter must flush each line; a run on a frozen tree hides its own list mistake for a day, so the floor needs its
alarm from the first hour; a fix merged while the climb runs proves nothing until a restart reads it live.

**Item 7's finish, as run (2026-11-08 16:26 UTC to 2026-11-09; the lead's worktree `m6-7-finish`, branch m6/7-finish, cut from master 9fa8352a).** The owner's three asks: the coin change, item 7's rows, a quick 1-20 sprint. Steps 1 and 2 merged first as 9409b17a (D111, D112, section 9's block "Item 7's finish"); the owner's cap (D112) came in by message during step 2 and was built before the sprint, so the sprint tests it from level 1.

**The sprint** (`cache/m6/7/live/finish-sprint`, not tracked; a fresh `Winnie`, `granted=0`, no fixes and no restarts by hand). 2026-11-08 18:00 to 2026-11-09 06:15 UTC, 736 minutes. **Gate A's bar is not met: level 19, not 20.** Level 6 at 18:05, 10 at 19:10, 15 at 22:19, 16 at 23:31, 17 at 01:26, 18 at 02:51, 19 at 04:27. The rung changes the ladder asks ran (outskirts, Burrow, Lodge). The Female Tusk was turned in live at 01:28 UTC (`quest: key=tusk-female done=yes xp=+104110 timer=saved`), so gate A's tusk is met again. Restarts 0, against gate A's 21; deaths 1, against 11: 2026-11-09 01:48 UTC, a Jungle Reaver on the road at 0xF9800019 during a supply trip, 20 hits for 94 damage at 65 maximum health. Verdict line: `level_start=6 level_end=19 kills=42 deaths=0 granted=0 armor_buffs=4:4:4:4 damage_factor=0.089 casters=2 rung_changes=15` (its `kills` and `deaths` are the last stretch's, not the night's).

**How it ended.** The launcher's hard timeout killed the client at 735 minutes (exit 124) in the middle of a walk. The 720-minute stop never fired: the rule reads only between cycles, and she had begun another cycle and a trip. Named `m6-minutes-stop-safe-point`.

**Rates.** Outskirts 71 kills an hour (441 in 6.2 hours; level 6 to 16); Burrow 35 (42 in 72 minutes; its floor 7); Lodge 17 (78 kills in 4.5 hours, its floor 75; gate A's best 100 to 120). The Lodge's figure leaves out kills on trips. Spend of the night's raises: Creature 15.2%, Max Mana 11.3, Item 10.4, Focus 10.1, War 9.9, Life 9.9, Self 9.5, Strength 7.4, Endurance 7.0, Max Health 4.9 (gate A's Strength 26.6 and health 18.7).

**Why the Lodge was slow: the lead's own change.** The Lodge's Mana Potion was set to trip at 1 in step 2, on the reading that potions were rarely drunk. She emptied them, and the counters sent her home nine times: 01:40, 02:41, 03:05, 03:21, 04:14, 04:50, 05:14, 05:38 and 06:12 UTC. The eight finished trips cost 119 minutes of the Lodge's 4.5 hours, and the 01:40 trip is the one the night's only death came on. All trips together: 195 minutes (learn 30, foci 12, potions 119, components and the rest 34). The change is undone (`trip_under` 0 again). Whether she would have reached level 20 without it cannot be proved; 19 to 20 was the one step left.

**What the night measured.** The pack-count line, fights by their peak of awake creatures within 24 m: 1 in 46 fights, 2 in 8, 3 in 13, 5 in 2, 6 in 3, and one each at 7, 8 and 9. The way out left a fight by recall for mana 10 times, with no death in the Lodge. Coin: the one real death left 7,286 pyreals on a corpse, and she left it (`leave-corpse:cell-unsafe`, a road cell on no ground's list); the safe-spot rule answered as built and no coin walk happened. Buffs skipped for want of components: 225 misses, led by level III of Life Magic Mastery 27, Strength 26, Invulnerability 22, and 18 each of Willpower, Quickness, Endurance and Coordination: the component rule (D115) answers these.

**Second sprint.** Recommended, because the miss came from a defect the lead added; the owner's call. The rules built after this sprint (D114, D115) and the undone trip have not been read live.

After the sprint, the rules D114 and D115 were built and the potion trip undone (3e5a6e75, `ManaOrder.PressLevelOf`, `ResupplyStation.ScrollKit.cs`); neither has run live. Final check on 3e5a6e75 (`slice-check -Changed`, 2026-11-09): build 0 warnings, 14,065 default passed, 0 failed, 6 skipped, every step PASS, `ok=true`; the baseline is now 14,065. The load-sensitive timing test `A_one_kilometre_plan_takes_low_single_digit_milliseconds` failed in plain `dotnet test` runs of the whole solution and passed alone; the slice check leaves that tier out. Spend by the meter: 82% at the finish's start, 90% (the stop line) at 2026-11-09 06:20 UTC, about 8 points; the build after the reset is not in that figure.

Mod installs: one, the coin rule, `-ModOnly` from the worktree (DLL b93f9853). Lessons: a change to a ground's trip line needs the rate of the thing it counts, read from the logs, before it ships; a monitor's filter chain must flush every stage (a `cut` swallowed the first rehearsal's lines); a level read from a cycle's end line is not a stall alarm when one fight runs for an hour.


**Items 8 and 14 and the player rules, as run to the merge (2026-11-09 to 2026-11-13 04:00 UTC; the notes `cache/m6/8/notes.md` and, from 2026-11-10 23:37 UTC when one agent took the coordinator's, item 8's lead's and the harness's seats, `cache/m6/takeover/notes.md`).** Merged to master on the owner's word before the items' gates (D134); what each still owes is in section 9.

- **Item 8** (branch m6/8): the six quests of D118 built into the climb (the Atlan Wand chain, the Minor Smoldering Stone, four fetches), the survival layer (D119-D123: one layer above every activity; the last resort for a failed way home; sell first, then buy), the fight's mana loop by the sum (M6 8 H). The long rounds on Qwinnie from 2026-11-09 (long3 to long7c) found the faults the reroll was made for: six deaths of a level-27 climber on 2026-11-11, one disease (no mana, no heal, no recall). Not seen live yet: the wand's chain, the stone, the fetches' rooms (the quests ran off, `-NoQuests`, during the sprint).
- **Item 14** (branch m6/14): the buff bot in the server mod (S0-S2), the bot's build order (S3: Item Enchantment, Mana Conversion, Creature Enchantment), health drinks and tinctures, the leather set, the bot's word `all` (28 lines, 2026-11-12), the eight armour banes, Gold and Pyreal Scarabs as town stock at Uziz, the purse (the Jeweler; the learn trip counts the trade notes), the den run and its rehearsal (parked, D130), then on 2026-11-13 the Abode from 40 (D128), the live bolt cap (D129), the Abode's kit to the Cave's standard (its `fight` block, potions, tinctures, scarabs, level VI components), and the walk round an object that only ignores collisions (the Uziz town marker, wcid 49606: three trips stood still at the Town Network's drop on 2026-11-13 03:44-03:53 UTC; fixed in `RoutePlay.StopsTheBody`, the physics' own rule). Not done: S4 (the bot's look, D126), S5 (the climbs with the bot on and off).
- **The player rules** (branch m6/15, the owner's rule of 2026-11-11): the autopilot casts only from the spell bar and drinks only from the hotbar.
- **The climb (the sprint, reroll 3):** a fresh Qwinnie from 2026-11-12 00:42 UTC; level 40 at 2026-11-13 01:00:56 UTC, two deaths (the Cave at 28, the Temple's road at 32). The Cave from level 22: 1.16 to 1.44 M XP an hour, 222 to 277 kills an hour. The Abode's first 21 minutes at level 40 (2026-11-13 04:09-04:30 UTC, Flame Bolt V): 58 kills, about 2.9 M XP an hour, hits on her 1 to 4 (the largest 10), health never under 104 of 156.
- **Spend:** the week of 2026-11-09 read 50% at 2026-11-13 04:40 UTC (the stop line 90%, reset 2026-11-16 10:00 UTC).

- **Slice BB** (live 2026-11-07 01:14 to 01:17 UTC: 47 hits and no bolt in the keeper's dance; the 23:58 death on the Lodge walk): the keeper no longer stands before a fight under fire (`autopilot fight-back:` line; the fight's needs are cut to the den), and the corpse trip is for need and safety (`handler: corpse trip:` line; `trip_abort=cell-unsafe` is new). Coin on a corpse is destroyed, so no walk for coin; `corpse_coin.min_item_value` (default 20,000 Value) sits beside `min_coin` in the ladder file.
- **Slice BC** (the bind step must not end a run; live `gateA-climb19`, 01:59 UTC): the bind took but Lifestone Tie was refused before the send (45 mana held, cost 50), the generic recovery tried the recall from the lifestone and was refused the same way, and the mana dance's 120 s cap stopped the run `failed:travel:recall-home`. Now `RestForSpell` rests in place until the spell's mana is held (one log line each side) before the tie and before the recall; a tie that cannot be cast or does not link is a logged `TODO(m6-bind-tie-mana)` with the station still ok (`tied=no`, `bound=yes`); a body already at home plans `TravelWay.AtHome`, not a recall from home. Holding all four foci changes Lifestone Recall to the foci formula (Copper Scarab and Prismatic Tapers, both outskirts stock lines). New: `m6-bind-tie-mana` (item 7, in code). `m6-recall-components-stock` stays open, narrowed to the full formula's Comfrey and Powdered Malachite (Cindrue sells both; no stock line, so no pack slot spent; the lead places it). Tests +5 default.
- **Slice BD** (no state's keeper stands under attack): one rule in one place (`AutopilotScenario.KeeperFight.cs`): in any state's keeper phase (Vitals, Peace) and any station's wait, on a ground, with a hostile on her, the keeper stops at once, she fights back until no hostile is in reach (stop reason `keeper-fight-back`) and the state resumes from its start; a logistics trip waits for the lull; a stop line with a hostile on her takes Lifestone Recall by name when payable, then the trip from home; a leave refresh ended by an attack is dropped. Evidence: `AutopilotKeeperFightTests` (26), the halted pass in `VitalsKeeperTests`, `FightAZTests`, `J3bStationTests`. The live case: gateA-climb20, 03:34 UTC, a counters trip asked with tuskers on her, thirteen leave lines each started and ended on a hit, no bolt, death at 03:37. The rule stands down by design for the retreat's way home, the urgent recall, the recall before a stop, a death not yet handled, a scripted death, the heal in place under the ground's retreat line, and a one-shot task.
  - New names: `keeper-fight-fix-phase` (the mana dance's fix phase runs a keeper pass with no hostile check; item 7), `keeper-fight-awake-read` (the read model holds no creature's target, so a sleeper in reach holds a state to the 45 s quiet cap; M7 unless a gate shows a cost). Other waits with no such check, named and not changed: the Trip state's way home through the travel seam, the audit's waits, the final settle, the death handler's stages (the corpse walk has its trip-fight), the build stations' regeneration wait.
  - Watch at the next gate, report-only: a lull needs no target within 20 m and no attack for 10 s; on a dense ground a counters trip may wait long; a trip held past a pack-full loot line is a reason to add a bound.

- **Placed by the owner (2026-11-08):** `m8-auto-equip-sets`, set-based auto-equip, placed M8. A draft exists in `cache/local-model/lm14-ladder-loot-equip/` (not checked by Claude).

- **New from the long round (the owner, 2026-11-09):** `m6-cast-before-stand` (after the long round): the spell effect fires before she has stood up. The log of the round reads the stand key sent, the change to magic mode confirmed 17 ms later, and the cast sent 23 ms after the key, with nothing checking that she is up. The fix is not a wait in the autopilot: it belongs in the client's own action path, the one a person and the autopilot both go through, so that a cast asked for while she is still getting up is held or refused the way retail's client does it and no caller can send it early (the owner: the client should enforce the correct behaviour on the player or the autopilot). The row's first job is to find in 11.6096 what retail's client really did, queued it until the motion finished or refused it, by function name and address, and in ACE `Player_Magic.cs` (`DoWindupGestures`, `DoCastGesture`), and copy that. Its second job: check whether other actions have the same gap, things the client lets the autopilot send that retail's client would have held or refused.

- **New from the long round's fifth start (item 8, 2026-11-10):** the quest timers file is named for the character's name, so a fresh `Qwinnie` read the last one's finished Lubziklan errand as `done-once` and never ran it (the long5 log: `due=no ... why=done-once ... last 2026-11-10T14:43:47Z`, a completion of the third start). Fixed in item 8: the file carries the character's guid and the run binds the timers to the character in the world, dropping another character's records by name (`QuestTimers.BindTo`, `JourneyContext.BindQuestTimers`; docs/session.md, the quest loader's persistence). New names: `m6-quest-timers-unowned` (a schema 1 file names no guid, its records are kept and logged; closes itself as files are rewritten), `m6-menu-timers-guid` (item 12: the menu's read-only load before a run does not check the guid), `m6-quest-timers-live` (the next long run: the `quest timers: check after: bound to` line read, and Lubziklan run by the fresh climber).
- **The sale before an errand (item 8, 2026-11-10; the local model's long4 idea, checked against the logs):** two of the long round's three errands began on a trip called for burden (138% and 141%) and both failed: one ran dry among the Cathedral's drudges, the other stuck at the hand-in with six jumps refused as too loaded; the one that began on a learn trip ran clean. A ladder errand called at the fight's trip now waits when her load is over the ground's trip line: the trip goes first and the errand is asked again from where the trip ends (`AutopilotScenario.ErrandWaitsForSale`, `ErrandAfterTrip`; the line `autopilot quest: key=<key>: check before: burden <n>% is over the trip line <m>%`). New name: `m6-errand-after-sale-live` (the next long run reads the line and the errand after the trip). Still open from the same logs: an errand is called only at a way home, so at levels 10 to 14 one comes up about every 100 minutes (`m6-errand-own-trigger`, item 9: whether a due errand should call its own way home).
- **Item 14, the buff bot (2026-11-11, D124-D127):** `m6-buffbot-real-character` (a real second character on its own account, driven by the headless client: real mana, components and fizzles; it needs an account and a word in CLAUDE.md's accounts rule, both the owner's); `m6-buffbot-stash` (the owner, 2026-11-15, deferred at the weekly budget: the climber keeps every Major, Epic and Legendary cantrip piece (`m6/14-keep-cantrips`) and needs a mule; the bot holds them: she tells it `holdfor <character>`, gives the pieces, and that character later tells it `giveback` (and `held` lists them); bot commands are single words, never spaces; the fix for never losing an item is the real bot of `m6-buffbot-real-character` (its own server connection, run by a thread in the client, so ACE saves what it carries like any player's), not a mod-held store; the mule is a second character on the climber's account (ACE allows one character online per account, `AuthenticationHandler` AccountInUse); the climber's home visit gives the pieces she does not wear; until then they stay in her pack, about 680 burden an hour of her 17,850); `m6-buffbot-price` (closed by D138: a credit a round from level 60, a Platinum Scarab a credit); `m6-buffbot-profiles` (more words than the tuskers': a profile by ground and by element); `m6-paid-bot-live` (D138: nothing of the paid bot is seen live: the hooks through the bot's own activation, the give and the credit line, a round's mana, fizzles and burns, the ten seconds, the walk to Archmage Cindrue through her front door and back, the trade and the state file across a restart); `m6-paid-bot-renew` (D138: at the stop's renew line of 45 minutes she pays up to four scarabs an hour, 62,000 pyreals; a renew line of its own for a paid round is the owner's call); `m6-gate-recall-castable` (a rung gate opens only when Lifestone Recall can be cast: the long round's first death, 2026-11-11, followed a recall refused for its components).
- **Item 14, the den run (2026-11-12, section 3.1):** `m6-den-run-live` (nothing of the run has been seen live: the route's walks are proved on the dats and the rules on the scripted server); `m6-den-run-drink-feet` (ACE lets a drink be drunk on the move; whether the client's body keeps its feet under the server's Drink motion is not measured); `m6-den-run-ladder` (the ladder file names no rung on `black-spawn-den-sho`: after the tie the den is reached by Primary Portal Recall, and which den the ladder's den rung plays is the ladder's to say); `m6-den-run-relaunch` (one attempt an order holds inside a run; the checkpoint keeps no mark of an attempt, so a relaunch with the switch is a new order); `m6-den-run-climb-verdict` (`tools/climb.ps1`'s verdict line is not taught the order's stop: the level up log's `autopilot done ... stop=den-run:tied` is the line to read); `m6-den-run-hub-doors` (the way inside the Desert March dungeon was planned with no server object: a door or a locked gate on it would be the hop's own named failure); `m6-den-run-other-dens` (the entrances 7242 and 7244 have no route in the travel file); `m6-den-run-tie-slot` (ACE keeps one primary tie: the den run's tie takes it from the ground that held it, and the next rung change to a tieable entry, the Temple 20-40's, ties there again and takes it back from the den; the autopilot's tie memory carries whichever came last. Keeping the den's tie needs a rule that the ladder does not tie elsewhere, or the secondary tie, which the catalog sells and no rule uses).
